}\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::lowest();\n    val_t energy_min = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n    \n    for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n        energy_sum += elem.current_energy;\n        flux_sum += elem.total_flux;\n        energy_max = std::max(elem.current_energy, energy_max);\n        energy_min = std::min(elem.current_energy, energy_min);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Validation results:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Energy sum: %.12f\\n\", energy_sum);\n    printf(\"  Flux sum: %.2f\\n\", flux_sum);\n    printf(\"  Energy range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", connections per element (for a 2D grid: 4 neighbors)\nconstexpr int MAX_CONNECTIONS = 8;\n\n// Material properties for energy transfer\nstruct Material {\n    val_t transfer_coeff;  // Energy transfer DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy\n    for (int i = 0; i < n_elems; ++i) {\n        world.elements_static[i].material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        world.elements_static[i].num_connections = 0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n    \n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    for (int x = 0; x < n_elems_root; ++x) {\n        for (int y = 0; y < n_elems_root; ++y) {\n            const int idx = x * n_elems_root + y;\n            ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n            \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n                \n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// Run simulation for n_iters This keeps std::vector::resize() from writing every page from the\n// master thread, so the parallel initialization loops below perform the first touch and\n// the pages end up on the NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct DefaultInitAllocator : std::allocator<T> {\n    using std::allocator<T>::allocator;\n\n    template <typename U>\n    struct rebind {\n        using other = DefaultInitAllocator<U>;\n    };\n\n    template <typename U, typename... OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root +    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = (size_t i = 0; i < elements.size(); ++i) {\n        // Simple hash combining energy and flux values\n        const uint64_t* e_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].current_energy);\n        const uint64_t* f_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].total_flux);\n        hash ^= (*e_ptr + i) * 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15ULL;\n        hash ^= (*f_ptr + i) * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL;\n    }\n    return hash;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Grid size (NxN bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    \n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n This keeps std::vector::resize() from writing every page from the\n// master thread, so the parallel initialization loops below perform the first touch and\n// the pages end up on the NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct DefaultInitAllocator : std::allocator<T> {\n    using std::allocator<T>::allocator;\n\n    template <typename U>\n    struct rebind {\n        using other = DefaultInitAllocator<U>;\n    };\n\n    template <typename U, typename...    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize elements and build connectivity. update.\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (int idx = 0; idx < n_elems; ++idx) {\n        const int x = idx / n_elems_root;\n        const int y = idx - x * n_elems_root;\n\n        ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n        elem.material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        elem.num_connections = 0;\n        for (int n = 0; n < MAX_CONNECTIONS; ++n) {\n            elem.connected_idx[n] = 0;\n            elem.connected_flux[n] = 0.0;\n        }\n        world.elements_dynamic[idx].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[idx].total_flux = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[idx].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[idx].total_flux = 0.0;\n\n        // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n        const int  // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n            if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                elem.num_connections++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// Run simulation for n_iters iterations\nvoid runSimulation(World& world, const int n_iters) {\n    const size_t n_elems = world.elements_static.size();\n    \n    for        const ElementDynamic& elem_dyn = world.elements_dynamic[i];\n            const Material& mat = world.materials[elem_static.material_idx];\n            \n            // Start with external flow\n            val_t total_flux = mat.external_flow;\n            \n            // Add flux from all connected elements\n            for (idx_t j = 0; j < elem_static.num_connections; ++j) {\n }\n        \n        // Swap buffers\n        std::swap(world.elements_dynamic, world.elements_dynamic_swap);\n    }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::lowest();\n    val_t energy_min = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n    \n    for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n        energy_sum += elem.current_energy;\n        flux_sum += elem.total_flux;\n        energy_max = std::max(elem.current_energy, energy_max);\n        energy_min = std::min(elem.current_energy, energy_min);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Validation results:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Energy sum: %.12f\\n\", energy_sum);\n    printf(\"  Flux sum: %.2f\\n\", const uint64_t* e_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].current_energy);\n        const uint64_t* f_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].total_flux);\n        hash ^= {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CedZtcKhabspvPa3YwG8K","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. The benchmark is now OpenMP-parallel, builds clean, and produces bit-identical output to the original binary.\n\n## Changes\n\n**`unstructured/unstructured.cpp`**\n- **Main loop** (`runSimulation`, unstructured.cpp:151): a single `#pragma omp parallel` region wraps the whole iteration loop, so threads are forked once rather than per iteration. Inside, `#pragma omp for schedule(static)` distributes elements and `#pragma omp single` performs the buffer swap. The implicit barriers of `for` and `single` give exactly the original read-after-write ordering between iterations. Buffer pointers are hoisted into `__restrict__` locals.\n- **NUMA first touch**: `resize()` on a `std::vector` value-initializes every element from the master thread, which would place all 2.4 GB (at n=4096) on one NUMA node. A `DefaultInitAllocator` (unstructured.cpp:21) turns that into default-init (no writes for these trivial structs), and mesh setup was merged into one `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` over the linear element index — the same partition the compute loop uses — so each thread first-touches, and later owns, the elements it will update. Both dynamic buffers are explicitly zeroed there.\n- `proc_bind(spread)` on both parallel regions, so the layout holds even when `OMP_PROC_BIND` is unset (636 ms vs 704 ms unbound at n=4096). Thread count is left entirely to the runtime/env.\n- Connectivity build now derives `x`/`y` from the linear index; neighbor visit order (and thus float addition order) is unchanged. Prints the thread count in the header.\n\n**`unstructured/CMakeLists.txt`**: `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` + link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. Executable name and flags unchanged. (I deleted the shipped `build/` dir — its CMake cache pointed at a different source path and refused to configure.)\n\n## Results\n\nVerified bit-identical `Result hash`, `-r` result block, and validation output against the original binary for `-n 512 -i 10`, `-n 999 -i 7`, `-n 37 -i 13`, and the edge cases `-n 1` (zero connections) and `-i 0`; also identical across 1/2/7/64/128/256 threads.\n\nn=2048, i=100 (`OMP_PROC_BIND=spread OMP_PLACES=cores`), 2×64-core EPYC 7763:\n\n| threads | 1 | 2 | 4 | 8 | 16 | 32 | 64 | 128 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| ms | 3887 | 2202 | 1078 | 521 | 284 | 168 | 126 | 111 |\n\nOriginal serial binary: 3923 ms — so ~35× at 128 threads, near-linear to 32.\n\nOne thing to flag: at large sizes (n=4096) scaling saturates around 32–64 threads (~15×, ~280 GB/s). The kernel is memory-bandwidth-bound — `ElementStatic` is 144 B per element while only ~40 B is live (4 of 8 connection slots used, 64-bit indices). Packing connectivity into a CSR-style layout would cut the streamed traffic roughly 3× and is result-identical, but it replaces the benchmark's defining data structure, so I left it alone; say the word if you want that version."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":578,"cache_read_input_tokens":50045,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":578},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bae7ae56-fc2d-4db2-8058-718b7cf7b5cd","uuid":"4ef14340-da45-4eb5-8d95-55e9b659505c","timestamp":"2026-09-01T23:13:56.573Z","request_id":"req_011CedZtbUcEmyJyxBMiTwLD"}
